## Phase73 — owner decisions (2026-11-05)

Gate 0 binds `main` at the `0.1.9` release commit `bca733bb` (tag `v0.1.9`),
whose natural exact-head CI `42090026718` attempt 1 accepted all 19 jobs; a
provisional Gate 0 on the Phase72 batch-2 integration tree came first, under
the owner's answer that let Phase73 start before Phase72 closed. On
2026-11-05 the owner accepted every Gate 1 recommendation; an item that would
add a new refusal needing its own approval is still asked separately. The
[Phase73 route](phases/phase-73/route.md) lists the slices.

| Item | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| D73.01 Conditional aggregates | `sum(1 for o in group if c)` counts and `sum(o.f for o in group if c)` sums the rows of this group that pass `c`; `group` names the group's rows. One generator has one meaning: `for o in <relation>` always reads the whole relation and stays only in `where any(...)`. The owner approved the spelling table separately the same day with its defaults: an empty conditional sum is `0`; aggregate queries without GROUP BY are accepted; grouped queries with a JOIN are refused in the first version with a hint; `any(...)` over a group is refused with a hint to the conditional count; MySQL refuses the conditional sum in Phase73. |
| D73.02 List `any` | `any(...)` over a list never becomes a second semi-join. |
| D73.03 Missing list | `len` and `any` of a missing list give `None`. |
| D73.04 Sum domain | List `sum` and the conditional sum take `Int` first; `Decimal` can follow compatibly. |
| D73.05 Nested-key equality | Ordered comparison: a nested GROUP BY or DISTINCT key is admitted only when the ORDER BY of the collected `let` ends in a key with a uniqueness proof (ties are refused), and elements are compared after decoding with `same_value`. |
| D73.06 Nested equality | Nested `==`, ORDER BY and set-operation dedup stay refused. |
| D73.07 Constants | `cutoff: Date = "2024-01-31"`: a Text literal is checked against the declared type; `Date` is admitted, `Decimal` not in the first version. |
| D73.08 Text and temporal comparisons | Phase73 starts no new check of Text compared with `Date`, `Timestamp` or `UUID`. |
| D73.09 `--set` | `--set NAME=VALUE` on `check`, `emit-sql` and `explain`; `migrate` refuses it; an undeclared name, a value of the wrong type, a repeated name or a combination with `--project` is a deterministic usage error. |
| D73.10 Window expressions | The slice lists its sub-forms for the lead's review; every other form stays refused. |
| D73.11 PIE-S2381 | Keeps its code and gains a hint that names the two-level route; no "group key fixes the multiplicity" rule is added. |
| D73.12 LIMIT 1 capture tie | Kept and documented, with no new code and no new refusal; an at-most-one grain fact waits for a consumer. |
| D73.13 Nullability v2 | Decided after the lead's read-only review. |
| D73.14 Parameter types on MySQL | All four new project parameter types go to the Phase74 MySQL catch-up. |
| D73.15 Release | Phase73, Phase74 and Phase75 ship together as one release, `0.1.10`, after Phase75 closes, with separate approval; there is no Phase73 or Phase74 release of its own. Each batch still publishes to `main` with natural exact-head CI, with no tag, GitHub pre-release or PyPI upload in between. Versions stay on `0.1.x` patches until about the end of Phase80, then `0.2.0`. |
| D73.16 Compiled `VERSIONS` | One change per phase, made by the root in the first slice, in commit order, that changes a compiled record; old bundles and saved jobs are refused with a typed failure, without migration. |
| D73.17 Joint-plan answers | The provisional answers J5–J8, J12 and J28 are confirmed. |
| D73.18 Budget | C180/F600/D2400/S11; at any cap the lead stops and asks. |
| D73.19 Phase71 deferrals | Multi-level collections, capture of a capture, nested carry or window values, and child recovery, R2 and sinks stay refused, each with a negative test. |

The same day the owner added four slices: S14, project aggregate windows,
with C15/F40/D260 more budget; S15, in which `compile_query` validates the
whole contract and then keeps only the sources the query reads; S16, project
aggregate type and expression-argument parity with single files; and S17,
`median(x)` and `quantile(x, q)` on PostgreSQL, refused on MySQL with a hint.
Single-file `count(<Bool>)` keeps its result and gains a warning that points
to the conditional count. Variance, `bool_and`/`bool_or`, first/last and
similar aggregates are Phase74 Gate 1 candidates. Windows CI is a separate
maintenance step, and `compile_queries(...)` goes to Phase75 with J18.

Correction (2026-11-07): D75.03 kept `compile_queries(...)` out of Phase75;
it is a Phase80 Gate 1 candidate. The paragraph above is unchanged.

## Phase74 — owner decisions (2026-11-05 and 2026-11-06)

Gate 0 binds `main` at the Phase73 completion commit `37143dcb` (tree
`d93f9393`), whose natural exact-head CI `42277807557` attempt 1 accepted all
21 jobs. It is the repair child of the Phase73 batch-3 head `b148fdee`, whose
natural CI `42272513354` failed only one Windows 3.12 test (a forced
comparable `ctime`, now POSIX-only); that head is kept and was not rerun. A
provisional Gate 0 on the Phase72 batch-2 integration tree came
first, under the owner's answer of 2026-11-05 that let the Phase74 lines start
beside Phase73. The Gate 0 probes are recorded as the rebind and the lanes'
witnesses: the emission, compiled and live-template witnesses of an imported
def and relationship on PostgreSQL and MySQL, and the lanes' runs over every
`schema_version = 2` test owner; there was no separate probe run. On
2026-11-05 the owner kept the joint-plan answers for Phase74 as recommended
and let its three lines start at once; the lead recorded the Gate 1 below as
the Phase74 plan recommends. The order of inlining (J24) is the lead's
architecture choice; it changes no public diagnostic order or location, so it
is recorded here and the owner is informed. The
[Phase74 route](phases/phase-74/route.md) lists the slices.

| Item | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| D74.01 Import and export items | In a `schema_version = 2` (EXPLICIT_MODULES) project, `def NAME` and `relationship NAME` are items of the bare and the parenthesized import and export lists: `export def pos, relationship OrderCustomer`, `from "models/a.pietto" import def pos as p, relationship OrderCustomer`. They bind in the callable namespace through the existing exporter and binder; there is no second resolver. Re-exports chain as for other kinds. A def or relationship that is not exported stays private. Wildcard imports, `constraint` and `derive` exports and qualified calls such as `a.pos(x)` stay refused. |
| D74.02 Identity | An import binds the declaring module's path and name; `as` renames only the local binding. A def or relationship is checked once, in its own module: an alias in a def signature keeps that module's meaning, and a body keeps the built-ins that module binds. An imported relationship keeps its module's parameter shapes and field types; the importer need not import those shapes by name. The SQL is byte-equal to the same declaration written locally. |
| D74.03 Inlining order (J24) | Imports are resolved first; then each module's defs are inlined in import-dependency order, as today. A body expanded in an importer is located at the call, and each argument use at the argument. An error in a def is reported once, at the def; call errors (PIE-S2395, PIE-S2399) are at the call. A failed def or relationship import or export item, or such an import along an import cycle (PIE-S2703), stops the project check before semantic analysis, as a def error does; calls of a blocked def or relationship add no further errors. A project without def or relationship items keeps byte-equal diagnostics. Expanding imported defs during semantic analysis was the alternative not taken. |
| D74.04 Collisions | No winner is chosen. Local collisions are unchanged (PIE-S2001 at the later declaration). A def or relationship import binding that collides with a local declaration of any kind, or with another import of its name, is PIE-S2706 at the import binding; an ambiguous name is reported at its first binding. The message carries the hint `import def pos as <name>` or `import relationship OrderCustomer as <name>`. A module's own def or relationship collides only with a def or relationship import, so `import source rows` beside a local `def rows` stays legal. A local def and a local relationship of one name keep today's call-site ambiguity. |
| D74.05 Built-in names | An import binding whose local name is a built-in function, `Float`, `Text` and `concat` included, is PIE-S2398 at the import item. No new shadow scope is added, and no built-in silently wins. A `def concat` stays exportable and is imported under another name. |
| D74.06 Hints and refused spellings | A call of a def or relationship that another module declares gets a PIE-S2103 hint that names the import to write, or the export and then the import when it is not exported. When no declaring module can be imported from, the `0.1.9` text stays. A qualified call such as `a.pos(x)` gets the same hint, ending `, then call pos(...)`. PIE-P1000 lists eight item kinds; a `*` item gets `Name each item.`, and a `constraint` or `derive` item gets `Constraint and derive stay in their module.` The release notes list each exact text. |
| D74.07 Package modules | A `def` or `relationship` item in a PACKAGE_ROOT package module is PIE-S2707, with the suggestion `Package modules do not support def/relationship imports yet.` The package and project owner decides on them once a named package case exists, at the latest as a Phase82 freeze input. |
| D74.08 Relationship rows (J9b) | A relationship parameter takes the rows of a table or query, matched by name: each field of the parameter shape needs an output column of the same name and type. Types are compared after alias expansion; an enum is compared by its declaration; `Decimal` precision and scale are not compared; extra columns are ignored. A column that may be None, by its type or because its nullability is unknown, does not bind a field that cannot be None. A column of unknown type, such as `None` alone, is compared by name only, as a def argument is. Rows from a LEFT JOIN stay nullable. Only a grouped query's GROUP BY key proves a key; the parameter shape's `unique` does not. Source rows still bind only by their declared shape. PIE-S2604 names the first field that does not match. In a project, a query whose output row is not known where the call is bound (a query with a JOIN of its own, read in a query with a JOIN or through `any(...)`) keeps the plain PIE-S2604 refusal; the same test written out is unaffected. |
| D74.09 MySQL catch-up (J12) | A form opens on MySQL only after a passing two-database differential (PostgreSQL 18.6, MySQL 8.4.12); otherwise it keeps its refusal, and the release notes list it. Phase74 opens the def and relationship imports and the table and query rows, whose SQL equals forms already open; project UUID ordering, with `max(a, b)` and `min(a, b)` of two UUIDs; the project aggregate forms Phase73 opened on PostgreSQL; a relation `order by` a finite `Float`; single-file `max(a, b)` and `min(a, b)` of two `Bool` values, an integer column as every computed `Bool` on MySQL; and the backward asof JOIN. `Timestamp` and `Date` `min` and `max` reopen in batch 2 as a follow-up of S04, after their differential rerun passed. These stay refused: checked signed 64-bit `Int` arithmetic, whose differential did not pass (MySQL error 1690 on a representable product); `string_agg`, because `GROUP_CONCAT` truncates silently at `group_concat_max_len`; `Float` set operations and DISTINCT, whose zero guard first needs a decision on the `Float` value contract; and the other forms the release notes list. Project `sum(Int)` and `stddev` of an `Int` stay Phase77 exceptions. |
| D74.10 Asof JOIN on MySQL | The approved Phase72 spelling is unchanged. On MySQL a single file writes the latest row as a LATERAL subquery, and a project keeps its existing lowering. A relation that also has a FULL JOIN stays refused on MySQL. This was the first slice to cut at a budget cap; its differential passed, so it stays. |
| D74.11 New aggregates | On 2026-11-05 the owner made these aggregates Phase74 Gate 1 candidates, and on 2026-11-06 approved their spelling table with its defaults. `variance(x)`, `pvariance(x)` and `pstdev(x)` take an `Int` or `Float` field, as `stddev` does, with Python `statistics` meanings. `all(c for o in group)` and `any(c for o in group)` test each row of the group as WHERE does; a row whose test is None counts as not True, so the result is never None. `first(x, order by k, id)` and `last(x, order by k, id)` need an `order by` of non-None direct fields that ends in a unique key; arg_max is written `first(x, order by v desc, id)`, with no function of its own; a None value at the chosen row gives None. `count_distinct(a, b, ...)` counts distinct combinations of rows where no field is None. `def` names `first`, `last`, `all`, `any`, `variance`, `pvariance` and `pstdev` become PIE-S2398, and `any(... for o in group)` in a SELECT item is PIE-S2334 when a relation named `group` exists; the release notes list both. MySQL refuses `first` and `last` in the first version; MySQL `string_agg` stays refused (D74.09). The four slices add C36/F110/D650. |
| D74.12 Deferrals (J9–J11) | Measure and metric definitions stay unscheduled. User-written table functions, the function/plugin SPI and PostgreSQL extension functions go to the Phase76 Gate 1, which takes or reroutes them. No new built-in table function is named; the first Gate 1 that names a case takes it, at the latest as a Phase82 freeze input. Cube/dbt export goes to Phase84, and the scalar-function effect catalog (D70.06) to Phase88. Wildcard imports, `constraint` and `derive` exports and qualified calls stay with the module owner until a Gate 1 admits one; each stays refused with a negative test. |
| D74.13 Joint-plan answers | The provisional answers J9–J11, J13 and J24 are confirmed, and the J12 scope is rechecked as D74.09 states. Under J13 and the owner's answer of 2026-11-05, Phase75 lines run beside Phase74 and replay onto its heads; Phase75 publishes under its own route after Phase74. |
| D74.14 Release | As D73.15 says: Phase74 has no release of its own; `0.1.10` carries Phase73, Phase74 and Phase75 after Phase75 closes, with separate approval. Each batch publishes to `main` with natural exact-head CI, with no tag, GitHub pre-release or PyPI upload in between. |
| D74.15 Compiled `VERSIONS` | S01–S06 change no compiled record: def and relationship items never reach a compiled bundle, and a call's compiled predicate record names its declaring module's template. The new `aggregate_value` function values of S09–S12 ride the unreleased `(7, 1, 7, 1)`, as Phase73 batch 3 did: no release carries those marks, and `0.1.10` is the next release. `VERSIONS` stays `(7, 1, 7, 1)` in Phase74. |
| D74.16 Budget | C170/F550/D2000/S12; at any cap the lead stops and asks. Live database runs count in D. The cross-module and row lines are never cut. The four approved aggregate slices add C36/F110/D650, for C206/F660/D2650/S12 in all; S is the scope-repair cap (D71.10) and stays S12. |

On 2026-11-05 the owner let all three Phase74 lines start at once, beside
Phase73; the root merges Phase74's grammar after Phase73's. The same day the
owner raised the cap on coding lanes to 14 and then 16; on 2026-11-06, asked
by the owner to use idle capacity, the lead raised it to 20. Each lane still
runs at `nice -n 19` with at most `pytest -n 2`, and one real-database run
at a time. On 2026-11-06 the owner also had Gate 2 run on the integration
host with 12 pytest workers, after a measured run, and dropped the split onto
Teach as no gain. On 2026-11-05 the owner pre-approved the push of each
Phase73–75 batch once Gate 2 passes on its exact tree and `main` has not
moved since the rebind: fast-forward only, with no tag, release or PyPI
upload; a CI failure stops the lead, and a repair child follows without a
rerun. These answers are no standing authorization beyond Phase73–75.

## Phase75 — owner decisions (2026-11-05 to 2026-11-07)

Gate 0 binds `main` at the Phase74 completion commit `24645b36` (tree
`22b2b05a`), whose natural exact-head CI `42316298753` attempt 1 accepted all
21 jobs. Its inputs are the Phase74
[completion audit](phases/phase-74/completion-audit.md), the
[Phase75 handoff](phases/phase-74/phase75-handoff.md) and the Phase74
[engineering lessons](references/engineering-lessons.md#phase74). A provisional
Gate 0 on the Phase72 batch-2 integration tree `9cd8007b` came first, under the
owner's answers of 2026-11-05 that let the first Phase75 slices start beside
Phase74 (J13). The lines were built on that tree and on the commits `bca733bb`,
`3d37449b` and `ac4d18ae`, and replay onto `main`. Their witnesses are recorded
as the rebind: byte equality of the compile API with `emit-sql --project`, the
relation sequence and single-file parity of the column view, `check --project`
output byte-equal before and after partial analysis, and the restriction law of
partial analysis against `[sources].exclude`. On 2026-11-05 the owner chose the
names of the compile API and the column view and accepted the other
recommendations of their design. On 2026-11-06 the owner answered the editor
questions and named a client. On 2026-11-07 the owner answered the Gate 1
questions with the lead's recommendations, except the client of the VS Code
acceptance: it is the owner's VS Code extension, which takes its diagnostics
from `pietto lsp`, and this replaces J21 (D75.13, D75.15). The
[Phase75 route](phases/phase-75/route.md) lists the slices.

| Item | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| D75.01 Relation columns (J17) | `pietto explain --project ROOT` on a `schema_version = 2` (EXPLICIT_MODULES) project lists the output columns of each table and query, in the order of `emit-sql --project --all`. A column has a name, a type `{kind, name}` and a nullability: `non_null`, `nullable` or `unknown`. An `enum` or `shape` type also has `module`, the module that declares it (B1 audit item B1-1, the lead's recommendation; the lead read the owner's answer of 2026-11-07, "everything else as recommended", as accepting it). An unknown nullability stays unknown. A Collect column has the kind `collection` and an `element` list. The JSON format is `pietto.project-relation-columns.v1`; the text output lists the same relations. The view selects no dialect, reads no emission contract and builds no IR. A parse or semantic error gives `ok: false`, `relations: null` and exit 1; a root, configuration or source-read problem exits 2. A column whose type the compiler did not prove, such as an untyped `None` (warning PIE-S2385), fails the whole document with `project_resource` and exit 2. The kinds `type_alias` and `unknown` stay in the vocabulary, as in single-file `explain`, although no project produces them today. Schema 4 output is unchanged; schemas 1 and 3 stay refused, with new messages. There is no new PIE code. |
| D75.02 Compile API (J18) | `pietto.compiler` gives `compile_sql` for one table or query and `compile_all_sql` for the `--all` batch. Both take the project root and return `SqlEmission(status, document)`. The bytes and the status equal those of `pietto emit-sql --project ... --format json` with the same arguments. A wrong Python argument type raises `TypeError` before anything is read; `UnicodeEncodeError` is the one other documented exception. Every other outcome is a returned document. An unexpected internal error propagates unchanged, with no public wrapper type. Each call reads its trusted inputs again; there is no cache. `compile_query` and `pietto.execution` are unchanged. The spec makes no thread-safety claim. |
| D75.03 Executable batch | An executable batch `compile_queries(...)`, raised by the external audit of `0.1.8` and placed beside J18 by the owner on 2026-11-05, is not added in Phase75. It is a Phase80 Gate 1 candidate. The import time of `pietto.execution` is a separate performance item. |
| D75.04 `fmt --project` (J15 d) | `pietto fmt --project ROOT` with `--check` or `--write` formats each selected module of a `schema_version = 2` project with the single-file layout; without a mode it is a usage error. A module that fails to parse or to format refuses the whole run, and nothing is written. With `--write`, a symbolic-link module, and every write failure the pre-pass can predict, refuse the run before any file is written. Each changed module is then replaced atomically, in module order; a module changed after it was read stops the run at that module, and the modules before it stay formatted. Only selected modules are written. Schemas 1, 3 and 4 are refused with a hint. The output is text only. |
| D75.05 fmt spellings (J15 a–c) | fmt writes a string with double quotes: `'abc'` becomes `"abc"`, escapes are adjusted, and the value is unchanged. It writes a one-line parenthesized import or export list without the parentheses and the trailing comma; a multi-line list keeps them. It drops a redundant `asc`, in every `order by` position, the `order by` of `first` and `last` included. Both spellings stay accepted, with no warning. fmt does not change `table` and `query`, `1_000`, one-line and block forms or chained comparisons. Its self-check compares tokens after the declared normalization. fmt output may change before the Phase82 freeze, with a release-note line. |
| D75.06 Header lines | Every header line (`pietto N`, `mode ...`, `dialect ...`, `encoding ...`) is refused with PIE-P1000 at its keyword, with a hint for that line, in every command and check mode and in dependency packages. Only `migrate` reads header lines: it deletes a line with no effect and refuses `mode loose` or `mode strict` with PIE-P1011, whose hint names `--check-mode`. PIE-P1012 is retired, and its code is not reused. The grammar keeps the header rule and its tokens for this refusal and for `migrate`; removing them is a Phase76 Gate 1 item and a Phase82 freeze input. The release notes say that a published dependency package with a header line fails to load until it is migrated. |
| D75.07 Editions, spellings and `migrate` (J14, J16, J22) | Pietto adds no syntax editions. Every equivalent spelling stays, with no warning and no removal: `table` and `query`, `1_000` and `1000`, trailing commas, and the one-line and block forms of a clause. `migrate` still writes only to standard output and never changes its input; there is no `migrate --write`. |
| D75.08 Hints and documentation | The hints for the eight implicit rules of the external audit D8 change message and suggestion text only; codes, locations, order and acceptance are unchanged. They name the accepted route for a `group by` that is not a list of names, a `date_trunc` unit, `round` digits, a scalar around an aggregate (PIE-S2310) and a plain `Decimal` in project emission (PIE-B1004). In the same S05 polish, the lead's implementation choice, PIE-S2105 names the `Decimal` rule that refuses Decimal times Int, a `Decimal` with a `Float` under `+`, `-` or `*`, and a sign on a `Decimal`, in its message and its JSON suggestion. `pietto emit-sql --help` names the project batch form. `docs/capabilities.md` is a hand-kept snapshot of each feature family in single files and projects on both targets; it is probed again when a family changes, and no test regenerates it. The fmt documentation gate covers every `pietto` block of `README.md` and `docs/*.md`. |
| D75.09 `pietto lsp` (J19) | `pietto lsp` speaks JSON-RPC 2.0 with LSP framing over standard input and output, using the standard library only; `--stdio` is accepted and ignored. It publishes `pietto check` diagnostics only: no backend refusals (PIE-B...), no hover, no completion. A diagnostic's message is followed by its suggestion unless the message already contains it (S07 polish, the lead's implementation choice). It uses full document sync and UTF-16 positions, sends the document version and drops stale results. Project roots come only from absolute paths in `initializationOptions.projectRoots`; there is no discovery. Every selected module of a root gets diagnostics, open or not. A file outside every root is checked as a single file. The CLI command list gains `lsp`. It is alpha. |
| D75.10 Editor buffers | An unsaved buffer has its own type, not the type of a trusted read. The check and module identity accept it. Project emission, compiled builds and package lineage refuse it by type. A buffer is never configuration and never enters a digest, a pin or a cache; nothing is written. |
| D75.11 Partial analysis (D70.06) | For the editor only, in a `schema_version = 2` project, a module whose read, parse, header, def import, naming or later check stops the project check, or that is in an import cycle, is left out together with every module that imports it, directly or through other modules, and the rest is checked again. There are at most five passes, one more than each stop stage that can hide a later one. Each module still checked shows exactly what `pietto check --project ROOT --format json` shows with the left-out modules in `[sources].exclude`. A root of another schema version stays all or nothing in the editor, and `pietto check --project` stays all or nothing, byte for byte. |
| D75.12 Editor-only messages | The server adds five messages without a PIE code, with source `pietto`: a file selected by two roots; a file open under two URIs; two selected files whose names differ only in letter case; a module not checked because a module it imports has errors (Information, at the import); and a partial document change. No root, buffer, URI or file is chosen as a winner. The diagnostic catalog is unchanged. |
| D75.13 Client, acceptance and extension (J20, J21) | On 2026-11-06 the owner named VS Code with a generic LSP client, so `pietto lsp` and partial analysis are protected Phase75 scope. On 2026-11-07 the owner replaced the generic client with the owner's own VS Code extension (repository `MianliWang/pietto-vscode-extension`): the extension takes its diagnostics from `pietto lsp`; it drops its adapter of private Pietto APIs and the hover, completion, definition, symbol, model and connection features built on it, which may return later on public contracts; it keeps highlighting, snippets and folding. Its changes are made in its own repository, with no tag and no Marketplace release. The VS Code acceptance runs with that extension, automated through the extension's own VS Code test host where possible: against a wheel built from the B1 candidate before the B1 push, and again against a wheel built from the B1 head; the completion audit records both. Hover, definition, completion, symbols, an editor fact format and format or migrate previews are not in Phase75; they stay unscheduled candidates. |
| D75.14 Deferrals and found limits | The column view leaves out source columns, schema-4 columns, richer type facts and single-file JOIN relations; the Explain-for-explicit-modules follow-up keeps the rest. The relaxations of the D8 rules (grouping by a computed `let` in a single file, a scalar around an aggregate, `round` of a `Float` with digits) and a check that the contract relation matches the source table string go to the Phase76 Gate 1. A project FULL or CROSS JOIN that is blocked with PIE-B1003 `scalar_meaning_requires_field_projection` by an unread `Timestamp` or `UUID` field stays refused; the Phase76 Gate 1 takes it, with the Phase77 Gate 1 for MySQL, and the `0.1.10` release notes list it. The fmt layout items of the Phase75 handoff (the gaps between the tokens of a line, a head comment paragraph attached to the first declaration) are not J15 rules and stay with the formatter owner. |
| D75.15 Joint-plan answers | The provisional answers J14, J16–J20 and J22 are confirmed: J17 and J18 as answered on 2026-11-05, J19 with the editor answers of 2026-11-06, and J20 with the named client. J21, which set the VS Code extension aside, is replaced on 2026-11-07: the extension takes its diagnostics from `pietto lsp`, and the VS Code acceptance runs with it (D75.13). |
| D75.16 Release | As D73.15 says: Phase75 has no release of its own; `0.1.10` carries Phase73, Phase74 and Phase75 after Phase75 closes, with separate approval. Each batch publishes to `main` with natural exact-head CI, with no tag, GitHub pre-release or PyPI upload in between. |
| D75.17 Compiled `VERSIONS` | Phase75 changes no compiled record, so `VERSIONS` stays `(7, 1, 7, 1)`. |
| D75.18 Budget | C120/F350/D1500/S10; at any cap the lead stops and asks. The line work before the route counts. Phase75 has no real-database run. S is the scope-repair cap (D71.10). The route has two batches: B1 is the route publication and S01–S07, with S05 in two commits; B2 is S08–S10. The second whole-tree test pass is inside these caps. |

The answers of 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-06 on lanes, Gate 2 and batch
publication (D74.13 and the paragraph after it) also hold for Phase75: each
lane runs at `nice -n 19` with at most `pytest -n 2`; Gate 2 runs on the
integration host with 12 pytest workers; each Phase73–75 batch push is
pre-approved once Gate 2 passes on its exact tree and `main` has not moved
since the rebind, fast-forward only, with no tag, release or PyPI upload. A CI
failure stops the lead, and a repair child follows without a rerun. These
answers are no standing authorization beyond Phase73–75.

## 0.1.10 release — owner decisions (2026-11-07)

| Item | Chosen rule |
| --- | --- |
| Version (D73.15) | `0.1.10`, the next patch after `0.1.9`, is one release of Phase73, Phase74 and Phase75. It refuses the header lines that `0.1.9` warned about (PIE-P1000). Users who pin `pietto~=0.1` or `pietto<0.2` receive it, so the [0.1.10 release notes](release-notes.md#aggregates-cross-module-reuse-and-editor-tools-public-alpha-release-notes--0110) list every new refusal and changed diagnostic. |
| Release commit | The last commit of Phase75 batch 2, after the S10 audit commit; it changes only version owners and documentation, and one Gate 2 covers the whole batch. |
| Title | The release notes are titled `Aggregates, cross-module reuse and editor tools public alpha release notes — 0.1.10`; the GitHub pre-release is "Pietto 0.1.10 public alpha". |
| Aggregate `function` names | The project JSON v2 aggregate `function` values `filtered_count`, `filtered_sum`, `count_distinct_tuple`, `group_any` and `group_all` keep their names. A later rename is a public format change that the release notes list. |
| VS Code | The documentation names the Pietto extension for VS Code `0.2.0` (repository `MianliWang/pietto-vscode-extension`), which shows diagnostics through `pietto lsp` and is not yet on the Visual Studio Marketplace; until it is, a generic LSP client or an install from the repository. The release does not wait for the Marketplace, which the owner publishes separately. |
| Windows | No claim that Pietto supports Windows: the compiler and CLI are verified on Linux x86-64, CI also runs the platform-neutral tests on Windows x86-64, and batch `emit-sql --project ROOT --all --output DIR` stays Linux-only. The package classifiers stay unchanged until Phase81. |
| README links | As for `0.1.9`: in the release commit every README link to a repository document points at the tag `v0.1.10`, including the nine links that point at `blob/main` on `main`; the next ordinary commit after the release restores those nine to `blob/main`. |

On 2026-11-07 the owner approved the publication chain: once Phase75
closes and Gate 2 passes on the final batch-2 tree with `main` unchanged
since the rebind, the batch-2 series, release commit included, is pushed by
fast-forward; after natural exact-head CI succeeds, tag `v0.1.10` and the
GitHub pre-release are created. The owner uploads to PyPI with the owner's
own token. If Gate 2 or CI fails, the lead stops and reports; a failed head
gets a repair child, not a rerun. `0.1.10` is published only when tag
`v0.1.10`, the GitHub pre-release and PyPI carry the accepted files; this
section publishes nothing.

## Phase76 — owner decisions (2026-11-07)

Gate 0 binds `main` at the `0.1.10` release commit
`8e7c43f1852b2c26318b3217268a1f7416f44965` (tag `v0.1.10`, tree `dceca22c`),
the last commit of Phase75 batch 2, whose natural exact-head CI `42343772631`
attempt 1 accepted all 21 jobs. Its inputs are the Phase75
[completion audit](phases/phase-75/completion-audit.md), the
[Phase76 handoff](phases/phase-75/phase76-handoff.md) and the Phase75
[engineering lessons](references/engineering-lessons.md#phase75). The Phase75
Gate 1 sent the D8 relaxations, the FULL and CROSS JOIN item and the contract
name check here (D75.14), and the Phase76 handoff sent the `Decimal` item
(Phase75 Gate 1 Q8). The lead asked eight Gate 1
questions (P76-Q1 to P76-Q8) together with a roadmap report on the state after
`0.1.10`, with its own questions Q1–Q32 and recommendations R1–R10. On
2026-11-07 the owner took every recommendation except one, kept the Phase80
interoperability with five libraries (see the next section), and so chose
option A of P76-Q1 to P76-Q8, with `0.1.10` released first. Later that day
the owner adopted the lead's review of an external roadmap rebase, with its
ten technical reminders (the table after this one), and, when Phase76
started, chose to simulate `%` of `Float` operands in S04 and extended the
lead's Phase76 authority to Codex. Later that day the owner fixed the core
version mapping and removed editor facts from the `0.2.0` gate. The Source column
names each answer's file in the lead's evidence and its question:
(a) `owner-answer-20261107-roadmap-0110-p76.json`, for P76-Qn and the
report's Qn; (b) `owner-answer-20261107-p75-gate1.json`, Phase75 Q8 and its
follow-up on the extension; (c) `owner-answer-20261027-docker-exception.json`
and `owner-answer-20261028-mysql-differential.json`, the local database
exception; (d) `owner-answer-20261103-interlude-decisions.json`, the unit of
D; (e) `owner-answer-20261105-batch-push.json`, the push conditions; (f)
`owner-answer-20261106-gate2-12.json`, the Gate 2 workers; (g)
`owner-answer-20261105-version-policy.json`, the version numbers; (h)
`owner-answer-20261107-chatgpt-rebase.json`, the review of the external
roadmap rebase; (i) `owner-answer-20261107-p76-start.json`, the start of
Phase76; (j) `owner-answer-20261107-devmode-perf.json`, the version, Gate 2
and Lean Gate v3 decisions. An item marked "inform" was stated to the owner as the lead's
decision and stands because the owner did not object. The
[Phase76 route](phases/phase-76/route.md) lists the slices.

| Item | Rule | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D76.01 Scope | Phase76 narrows the roadmap row "PostgreSQL deep adaptation" to the PostgreSQL gaps users meet today. Batch 1 carries the E1–E4/E6 forms of S01–S03, a caller CA for PostgreSQL connections (D76.07) and the qualified MySQL forms of S07 (D76.09). Under the handoff fallback, S04/S05 carry their remaining numeric and Text-default work into batch 2 before the semantic changes of D76.04 and D76.05, the closeout and an audit-only slice. Not taken: native JSON columns as the main line (a new type, three or four more slices, no named user), or a minimal phase of only E1, `Decimal`, D8 and the CA. | (a) P76-Q1 A, Q8; (i) Q1 |
| D76.02 PostgreSQL project gaps | The lead decides E1–E10; each opens on PostgreSQL after a real-database run and otherwise stays refused with its code. E1: a project FULL or CROSS JOIN checks only the JOIN ports the relation reads, so an unread `Timestamp` or `UUID` field no longer blocks it with PIE-B1003 `scalar_meaning_requires_field_projection`; a read port keeps today's rule. E2, E3: a query whose WHERE calls a def that inlines to a conditional, read by a JOIN, and a scalar call inside an aggregate argument over JOIN rows, such as `count(lower(r.label))`. E4, E6: `Float` field arithmetic and negation under the finite binary64 law, with the checked-overflow evidence that `Float` `sum` and `avg` use (PostgreSQL raises 22003 on overflow and on underflow to zero), and `Float` window, FULL JOIN and distribution-result keys under the `Float` value contract of `language.md` (`-0.0 == 0.0`, and a merged key may show either zero sign). E5, E7: `/` of `Int` and `Float` operands with the meaning `language.md` states (an `Int` `/` truncates toward zero; the smallest `Int` divided by -1 gets checked 22003 evidence), `%` of `Int` operands, and a `Text` default of `lag` and `lead`. A zero divisor of `/` or `%` raises the PostgreSQL error 22012, while MySQL `/`, `DIV` and `%` return NULL, as `language.md` states. `%` of `Int` operands keeps the remainder that single files emit today on both targets: its sign follows the dividend (`-7 % 2` is `-1`, where Python gives `1`); `language.md` does not say so yet, and S04 states it there and in the release notes. `%` of `Float` operands, which the check refuses today with PIE-S2105 because PostgreSQL has no `float8` remainder, is simulated in S04 (owner, 2026-11-07; the draft had kept it refused): S04 states its meaning in `language.md` and the release notes (its sign, a zero divisor, ±0 and the finite binary64 law) before its run, and opens it in single files and projects only on a target where its run proves an exact lowering; on any other target it stays refused with a fixed code. E8: a `Decimal` compared with a literal, only if Gate 0 confirms that the check reads the literal as its exact decimal digits. E9: `Decimal` plus or minus `Int`, and `Decimal` values of different scales, exact, with precision and scale from closed formulas (an `Int` read by its physical width, `Decimal(19, 0)` for `int8`; scale `max(s1, s2)`) and bounded by the 65-digit chain. E10: `Decimal` `sum` with the carrier `Decimal(p + 19, s)`, admitted only while `p + 19 <= 65`. S05 must close the output range with an exact checked numeric cast or an actual source-cardinality proof; a returned-row limit is not such a proof. | (a) P76-Q1, inform; (i) Q1 |
| D76.03 Not taken | `Decimal` division and `avg` stay refused: they have no scale rule (Pietto lead, then the owner; latest the Phase82 freeze input). Project `matches(...)` stays refused: there is no regex contract between PostgreSQL and Python (Pietto lead; latest the Phase82 freeze input). A `Float` beside an `Int` literal in a project stays refused (owner, 2026-11-04). `Int` arithmetic wholly outside signed 64 bits stays PIE-B1002, `Float` `median` and `quantile` stay PIE-B1004, and UUID `min` and `max` stay PIE-S2314. | (a) P76-Q1, inform |
| D76.04 `Decimal` times `Int` | `Decimal * Int` with the `Int` on either side, and a unary `-` or `+` on a `Decimal`, are accepted; the scale is unchanged. A `Decimal` with a `Float` stays PIE-S2105. Single files emit on PostgreSQL, and on MySQL after their two-database run passes. PostgreSQL projects emit with the result type `Decimal(p + 19, s)`: the digits of a product add up, and an `int8` counts as 19 digits; it is admitted only while `p + 19 <= 65`, and a sign keeps `(p, s)`. MySQL projects go to Phase77. The single-file `sum(amount * n)` that passes the check and gets PIE-B1000 is fixed with it. The operator matrix, the `Decimal` contract and `language.md` change with it, and the release notes list each changed text. Not taken: keeping the refusal, or deciding the `Decimal` division and `avg` scales now. | (a) P76-Q2 A, Q9 |
| D76.05 D8 relaxations | (a) A single file accepts `group by` and `select` of a computed `let`, by the rule projects use. (b) First version: a scalar function or arithmetic may wrap direct aggregates in one query, with group keys and literals, such as `coalesce(sum(x), 0)`, `sum(a) * 100 / count()` or, in a single file, `round(avg(amount), 2)` for a `Decimal` amount (a project `Decimal` `avg` stays refused, D76.03). It holds in single files and PostgreSQL projects. Its acceptance test is that the one-query result equals the result of today's two-query route, which stays accepted (J16). The refusals of PIE-S2310, PIE-S2102 and PIE-S2329 that these forms meet go, their hints change again, and the release notes list each text. (c) `round` of a `Float` with digits is not in Phase76: Python rounds the binary value, PostgreSQL has no `round(float8, int)` and MySQL follows the C library. It is a Phase80 Gate 1 candidate and a Phase82 freeze input; until then `round(avg(price), 2)` holds only for a `Decimal` price in a single file, and an `Int` or `Float` average needs it. Each MySQL single-file form opens with its two-database run; the MySQL project form of (b) goes to Phase77. | (a) P76-Q3 A, Q9 |
| D76.06 Contract names and PIE-S2104 | No refusal is added when a contract relation differs from the source table string: in a project the contract is the only authority for physical names, and mapping one source to another table per environment is a real use; the Phase75 documents state the rule. The PIE-S2104 before PIE-S2310 on `round(avg(x), 2)` stays with the diagnostic review; D76.05 (b) removes it for the forms it accepts. | (a) Q9, inform; (b) Q8 |
| D76.07 PostgreSQL CA | `PostgresAccess` gains an optional `ca_file`. When it is given, both PostgreSQL routes (`postgres_rows`, `postgres_adbc`) verify the certificate chain and the host name (`verify-full`); a route that cannot is refused with a typed error before connecting and never ignores the file. When it is absent, behaviour is unchanged (`sslmode` `require`, or `disable` on a loopback address). A supplied CA is the only explicit trust input for this path: no environment variable or default certificate file is read by Pietto. The support matrix claims its first PostgreSQL TLS profile. Not taken: a required `ca_file` as `MySQLAccess` has, which would break callers that use `require`. | (a) P76-Q4 A, Q11 |
| D76.08 Bounded reads | `ExecutionLimits` is the bounded-read contract for PostgreSQL rows: each batch has at most 4,096 rows and 8 MiB; an attempt delivers at most 1,048,576 rows and 64 MiB, with `max_bytes` also counting qualifying profile and catalog reads; `seconds` is the 20-second attempt deadline; and PostgreSQL limits each statement to 10 seconds. The deferral from Phase69 is closed with no new contract. | (a) P76-Q4 A |
| D76.09 MySQL in Phase76 | (1) Single-file constants and `--set`, and `now()` as `UTC_TIMESTAMP(6)`, open on MySQL after a byte replay: the statements of their passed runs (18 of 18 flags; 33 checks) are emitted again from the candidate tree and compared byte for byte, and only changed statements run again. (2) The MySQL single-file forms of D76.04 and D76.05 ride the same two-database runs; a form that does not pass stays refused with its code and the target Phase77. (3) The MySQL CROSS JOIN of E1 rides the S01 run and opens if it passes; this replaces the Phase77 routing of D75.14 for CROSS only, and MySQL FULL JOIN stays `mysql_full_join_approved_non_support`. The capability page gains its rows for `string_agg` and the typed project parameters. Every other MySQL form stays refused for Phase77; the release request after Phase76 lists the forms beyond the cadence under J12 (3), among them checked `Int` arithmetic, the project `concat` and the project forms that no Phase74 slice took. Not taken: Phase76 on PostgreSQL only, or more MySQL project forms now. | (a) P76-Q5 A, Q10, R2 |
| D76.10 MySQL lag | The J12 exceptions stay (project `sum(Int)` and `stddev` of an `Int`, Phase77; D73.17), and so does D71.05 (MySQL nested results, Phase77). MySQL may trail PostgreSQL by about two to three phases: the owner's rule of 2026-10-28 said releases, the joint-plan answer J12 counts phases, and the lag stays counted in phases. | (a) Q7 |
| D76.11 PostgreSQL-only subjects | Rerouted, not taken. PostgreSQL extension functions (the pg_trgm 1.6 and pgvector 0.8.6 catalogs exist since Phase57 but are not callable, PIE-S2103) and the function/plugin SPI with user-written table functions are unscheduled until a Gate 1 with a named use case takes them; their latest point is the Phase82 freeze input, and the 1.0 support matrix lists them as not supported if none does. Database-native array and JSON columns and the `postgres_adbc` nested route are Phase80 Gate 1 candidates; their latest point is the Phase82 freeze. This settles D74.12 and the Phase71 routing to Phase76. Not taken: the three pg_trgm similarity functions with a public extension declaration, or read-only JSON columns. | (a) P76-Q6 A, Q8 |
| D76.12 Header grammar and the extension | The `Header.mode` that `pietto.semantic.analyze` reads on a hand-built AST is deleted. The header rule and its tokens leave the grammar only if `migrate` and the per-line PIE-P1000 hints stay byte-identical to `0.1.10`; otherwise they stay until the Phase82 freeze (D75.06). A grammar change runs the generation and reproducibility checks. A slice that changes the grammar or the built-in catalog carries the sync of the owner's VS Code extension, which the lead makes in the extension's repository: a commit and a fast-forward push to its `main` after an independent review and the extension's own tests, with no tag and no Marketplace release. | (a) P76-Q1, inform; Q17; (b) follow-up; (i) Q5 |
| D76.13 Compiled `VERSIONS` and patch compatibility | After `0.1.10` the first Phase76 slice that changes a compiled record, if any does, moves `VERSIONS` once from `(7, 1, 7, 1)`, and later slices ride it until the next release (D73.16). That release then refuses `0.1.10` bundles and saved jobs with a typed failure, without migration. Until 1.0 a `0.1.x` release may carry such breaking changes; each release lists them at the top of its notes with a pin to the previous exact version, and keeps recompiling, reading saved results and running again apart. A migration waits for the Phase82 compatibility decision. | (a) P76-Q1, inform; Q29; (h) reminder 7 |
| D76.14 Release | No Phase76 commit reached `main` before the `0.1.10` release commit; its lines worked in worktrees and evidence folders. Phase76 batches publish to `main` with no tag, GitHub pre-release or PyPI upload in between. The Pietto core release after Phase76 and Phase77 is `0.1.11`; Phase78–80 lead to `0.2.0` only once its gate is met. A tag, GitHub release and PyPI upload still require separate approval, and PyPI remains the owner's action. Not taken: Phase76 batch 1 on `main` before `0.1.10`, which would have put it into `0.1.10`. | (a) P76-Q7 A, Q13; (g); (j) versions |
| D76.15 Pushes and lanes | Each Phase76 batch push is pre-approved once Gate 2 passes on its exact tree and `main` has not moved since the rebind: fast-forward only, with no tag, release or PyPI upload. A CI failure stops the lead, and a repair child follows without a rerun. While the owner is not using the computer, Gate 2 runs on the integration host with at most 18 pytest workers on CPUs 0–19; when the owner resumes use, it returns to 12 workers on CPUs 0–11. Lanes never commit; up to twenty concurrent isolated work items (implementation and independent read-only review) run at `nice -n 19`, and a lane starts a pytest run only while the one-minute load is below 10 and more than 6 GiB of memory is available. When Phase76 started, the owner extended every Phase76 authority in this record to Codex when it acts as lead: the batch pushes, the database exception with its TLS container (D76.16), the local Gate 2 and the extension pushes (D76.12), under the same rules. | (a) P76-Q7 A, Q13, renewing (e) for Phase76; (f); (i) Q5; (j) Lean Gate v3 and worker cap; the lanes: the parallel plan of the P76 questions, inform |
| D76.16 Database runs | The local database exception (pinned PostgreSQL 18.6 and MySQL 8.4.12 images, network off, at most two hours each, every resource ours and cleaned up) holds for Phase76. A campaign holds its engine-specific lock, `/tmp/claude-1000/pietto-docker-campaign-postgres.lock` or `/tmp/claude-1000/pietto-docker-campaign-mysql.lock`, and runs at most one container for that engine; one PostgreSQL and one MySQL container may run together. Each container uses its fixed image ID, `--pull=never --network none`, an owned `pietto-p76-*` name and `docker rm -f -v` cleanup. The exception covers one PostgreSQL container with TLS on for D76.07: its test CA and server certificate are generated locally with no network and mounted read-only, and the private keys stay in the run directory. | (a) P76-Q8 A, Q12; (c); (i) Q5; (j) Lean Gate v3 |
| D76.17 Budget | C150/F450/D10000/S12, cumulative (the owner raised only D from 2200 to 4400 and then to 10000 on 2026-11-07 Toronto time, `owner-answer-20261107-codex-p76-d4400.json` and `owner-answer-20261107-codex-p76-d10000.json`; all prior usage remains counted); at any cap the lead stops and asks. D counts one per diagnostic command, and a loop inside one command counts once; this unit holds for every phase. Database runs, and the probe and design lines before the route, count. S is the scope-repair cap (D71.10). The route has two batches: B1 is the route publication, roadmap and Lean Gate v3 work, then S01, S02, S03, S06 and S07; S04/S05 carry into B2 before S08–S12 under handoff §0b.3. The scope remains twelve slices and all prior work counts. | (a) P76-Q7 A, Q32; (d) |
| D76.18 Documentation in batch 1 | The route publication commit records the `0.1.10` publication, points the README links that the release commit pinned to `v0.1.10` back at `main`, adds the Phase75 contracts to the `AGENTS.md` lists (the relation column view, the Python compile API, `pietto lsp` and the capability page) and drops its clause about history-named owners until the second whole-tree pass. A documentation commit after it cleans the roadmap: the roadmap keeps its forward-looking parts, its stale statements are fixed, and it gains the editor work package, the Phase82 freeze ledger, the content of `0.2.0`, the rules after 1.0 and the tentative rows 98–106 (next section). The same commit states in README and the project guide that batch `--output DIR`, written only on supported Linux filesystems, is not supported on Windows or macOS, which use batch stdout, a single `--output FILE` or `pietto.compiler.compile_all_sql(...)`, which returns the same batch document for the caller's own code to write; options with a Windows directory writer stay with Phase81. | (a) Q14, Q15, Q16; (h) reminder 9 |

The owner adopted the ten technical reminders of the lead's review on
2026-11-07 (source (h)). Each binds Phase76 as the third column says; a later
receiver in the fourth column is the plan of 2026-11-07, and that phase's
Gate 1 confirms it.

| # | Reminder | In Phase76 | Later receiver |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Each E item states its semantics, above all a zero divisor and ±0 | D76.02: PostgreSQL raises 22012 for a zero divisor of `/` and `%`, MySQL returns NULL; `Float` keys follow the `Float` value contract; the S03–S05 runs witness each | the MySQL forms of E4–E10 and the `Float` ±0 contract for merged zeros (Phase77) |
| 2 | `round(avg(x), 2)` holds only for a `Decimal` `x`; an `Int` or `Float` average still needs `round` of a `Float` with digits, so an example names its types and is not advertised as general | D76.05; the S10 examples name their types and use forms such as `coalesce(sum(qty), 0)` in projects | `round` of a `Float` with digits: the Phase80 Gate 1, the Phase82 freeze at the latest |

## Roadmap after 0.1.10 — owner decisions (2026-11-07)

The same answer (a) settled the roadmap after `0.1.10` as the lead
recommended, except that Phase80 keeps its interoperability with five
libraries. The answer (h) to the lead's review of an external roadmap rebase
then took the editor line out of Phase80, defined the local path of `0.2.0`,
numbered the tentative candidates 98–106 and adopted the ten technical
reminders above; where it differs from (a), (h) holds. The batch-1 roadmap
cleanup of Phase76 writes these rules into the [roadmap](roadmap.md). Sources
are as in the Phase76 section. The answer (a) also settled B1-1, as the lead
read it and stated back to the owner: the `enum` and `shape` type objects of
the relation column view gain a `module` field in Phase75 batch 2, which
D75.01 records.

| Item | Rule | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Phase80 | Phase80 keeps pandas, Polars, NumPy, SciPy and Matplotlib interoperability and the Python user surface; the recommendation to narrow it to a Python user surface, with NumPy, SciPy and Matplotlib as examples only, was not taken. Its Gate 1 candidates stay: `compile_queries(...)` (D75.03), database-native array and JSON columns and the `postgres_adbc` nested route (D76.11), `round` of a `Float` with digits (D76.05) and the Arrow data-analysis example (J25). The editor line is no longer part of it (next row). | (a) Q17, Q18, R4, R5; (h) 1 |
| Editor line | The editor line is its own work package beside Phases 76–80, tied to none of them, and starts when the owner approves its Gate 1: the core semantic owner provides the facts and the extension consumes them. Its first public contract is a spec page `docs/spec/lsp-diagnostics-v1.md`. It sets an editor latency budget, deciding whether the linear analysis of layered JOIN DAGs and the FD sharing of PMS-6 come first, and a public editor-facts contract, which first weighs extending the semantic metadata artifact v1 before a new format; then it adds hover, definition and symbols on that contract, and the extension's model view is rebuilt on the column view. The VS Code extension versions apart from Pietto, with a minimum Pietto version that its own checks enforce: `0.2.0` stays, small changes ship as `0.2.x`, `0.3.0` comes only with a user-visible editor feature, and no Marketplace pre-release channel is used. It gets a CI in its repository, which needs its own authorization, and its Marketplace timing is the owner's. Each Pietto phase that changes the grammar or the built-in catalog carries the extension sync (D76.12), and the sync script stops reading the private `pietto.parser_api._parse_migration_source`. | (a) Q17, R4; (h) 1; (i) Q3; the semantic metadata artifact: the lead's plan review of 2026-11-07, inform |
| Third dialect | Before the first Gate 1 of Phase78 or Phase79, a separate ARCHITECTURE_DECISION chooses how the new backends are built: small hand-written target adapters with sqlglot as a development-only oracle, or sqlglot dialect families as a runtime dependency of the new backends only, with the PostgreSQL and MySQL emitters kept and Pietto still deciding the SQL bytes and semantics; it states the dependency, pinning, byte-stability and error-ownership costs of each, and decides whether DuckDB comes before SQLite. Rows 78 and 79 stand until then. | (a) Q19, R6; the two options: the lead's plan review of 2026-11-07, inform |
| `0.2.0` and later versions | `0.2.0` requires PostgreSQL and MySQL within the MySQL cadence, one local analysis path (the limited DuckDB path of Phase79, not a complete third dialect) and the Phase80 interoperability with five libraries and its Python surface. It does not require editor facts; the editor ships on its own extension versions. The Pietto core releases Phase76 and Phase77 together as `0.1.11`, then Phase78–80 as `0.2.0` when this gate is met. The extension's versions do not follow this line. | (a) Q20, R8; (h) 2; (j) editor_facts_gate, versions |
| After 1.0 | Phase82 freezes concrete semantics and compatibility rules: each public contract states what a `1.x` release may add and what stays, and an addition that can break a working program or reader, such as a new reserved name or a JSON field, is a compatibility change. A breaking change waits for `2.0`. Phases 84–90 and the tentative 91–106 work inside those rules. | (a) Q30; (h) reminder 5 |
| MySQL | The `Float` ±0 contract for merged zeros (P25, P5) is on the Phase77 Gate 1 agenda, with a lead proposal built on the existing guard evidence. ## Phase77–80 and editor continuation — owner decisions (2026-11-08)

The owner made these six decisions in the continuation's Gate 1 questions
and approvals. They settle the choices below, not an unrecorded budget,
database campaign, new numeric law, publication or release.

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D77.01 Scope | Keep the complete 27-slice Phase77 route, including S21–S24 nested results. | The alternative that separates the nested work into another work package was not selected. Exact numeric and nested qualification still precedes opening each form. |
| AD78.01 Backend architecture and order | Build small Pietto-owned target adapters; use sqlglot only as a test oracle. Deliver the limited DuckDB path before SQLite. | Existing PostgreSQL/MySQL emitters keep their SQL-byte and semantic authority. No runtime sqlglot dependency or blanket new-dialect support is introduced. Phase numbers and the five-library Phase80 outcome stay unchanged. |
| ED.01 Feature priority | Prioritize hover, go-to-definition and document symbols after the diagnostic contract and core-owned editor facts. | The model-view rebuild follows later. Existing semantic metadata must be assessed before a new facts format; this choice does not freeze that format or an editor latency budget. |
| M77.01 Dependency maintenance | Adopt the reviewed PyArrow `26.0.1` plus Psycopg/Psycopg-binary `3.3.8` maintenance candidate; `3.3.8` replaces the original #110 proposal of `3.3.7`. | Local tests and package smoke do not grant database qualification; the selected routes still need fresh evidence. Historical `26.0.0` / `3.3.5` evidence stays unchanged. |
| D77.02 Execution and budget | Proceed with the complete 27-slice Phase77 scope under cumulative `C280/F850/D4000/S12`. The maintenance and Phase77 validations may use the fixed PostgreSQL 18.6 and MySQL 8.4.12 images locally without network access, at most one owned container per engine and two hours per campaign, with the corresponding Gate 2. | Engine locks, pinned images, owned cleanup, exact input/oracle evidence and failure preservation remain required. No commit, push or release is authorized. |
| ED.02 Facts contract | Use a core-process ephemeral snapshot invalidated with document versions, exposing only standard LSP hover, definition and document symbols. Existing metadata and relation-column formats stay unchanged. | Return a definition only from exact core binding evidence; unknown/conflicting facts yield no result. Requests perform no new parsing or compilation. No independent editor-facts artifact or persistent cache is introduced. |

## Phase78–80 and editor core Gate 1 — owner decisions (2026-11-10)

On 2026-11-10 (America/Toronto) the owner approved the Gate 1 of four packages
with the recommended answers of the lead's Gate 1 decision sheet: the editor
core work package E-C, Phase79 DuckDB B1, Phase80 interoperability and Phase78
SQLite B1. The extension lane (sheet Q4) was not approved. Its items are X0,
landing the extension's uncommitted seven-file patch with a first extension
CI; X1, MySQL single-file and per-module project previews; X2, a model view
over the column view; X3, actions per diagnostic code and a restart on
configuration change; and T1, the interim extension-side target-readiness
(PIE-B) view of Q11. X1 and X3 are developed only locally under EX.01; the
other items, and extension CI and pushes, stay planning-only. The sheet
itself is an owner-held working record outside Git. PL.01–PL.06 state once the
answers the four packages share (sheet Q1–Q3 and the lead's rulings on them).
Each route cites them by id and keeps only its own rows. A row marked "lead,
inform" is the lead's choice. It is stated to the owner for objection before
the point the row names. Routes: [E-C](editor/ed03-route.md),
[Phase78](phases/phase-78/route.md), [Phase79](phases/phase-79/route.md),
[Phase80](phases/phase-80/route.md).

It consumes `ProjectSQLPlan`, with dispatch at the CLI, `compiler._emit` and `compile_query`. The PostgreSQL/MySQL pipeline never receives `"duckdb"` or `"sqlite"`. ### Phase78 SQLite B1

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D78.01 SQLite B1 scope and surface | Sheet Q7 A′. B1 is single-file `pietto emit-sql FILE --dialect sqlite`, in text and JSON, over a flat subset: `where`, `select`, `order by`, `limit`, `group by`, `count`, `min` and `max` on `Int`, `Text`, `Bool` and finite `Float` fields, with no arithmetic. Its one source spelling is `sqlite.table("name")`, which names a bare table. CLI JSON v1 admits `"sqlite"` as a successful `dialect` value, and new SQL and JSON goldens follow the golden-fixture policy. | B1 has no execution route. It reads no caller `.db` file, which needs its own trust Gate 1. It has no project mode: `emit-sql --project --dialect sqlite` and `compile_query(dialect="sqlite")` keep their refusals, and `pietto.sql` exports no SQLite emitter. Schema-qualified or attached names, JOINs, windows, constants and `--set`, `Decimal`, `Date`, `Timestamp`, `UUID`, nested values and `LIKE`-style text functions stay refused. `semantic/catalog.py` has no connector entries today. If S01 still finds `sqlite.table` to be a built-in catalog change, its extension sync waits for the separate extension-repository authorization (sheet Q4, not approved). Not taken (SQLite draft Q1): project mode first, a public `sqlite_rows` route, and Arrow data copied into `:memory:`. |
| D78.02 SQLite B1 value rules (lead ARCHITECTURE_DECISION, inform) | B1 states its physical premises as asserted: `STRICT` tables, the default BINARY collation on every column, UTF-8, and `Bool` stored as INTEGER 0/1. Arithmetic is refused. The refusal is the fail-closed default and needs no evidence: the SQLite draft's unauthorized probe, which is not evidence, saw the build turn a signed-64 overflow into REAL and a Float overflow into an infinity. ORDER BY writes `NULLS FIRST`/`NULLS LAST` explicitly, so NULL placement equals the PostgreSQL emitter's for the same source. | The source is the SQLite draft's Q4 (first option), not the owner's sheet. The lead states it to the owner as inform, at the latest before S02 seals. No Pietto law changes; a premise that would change observable behaviour beyond current rules becomes USER_DECISION_REQUIRED. Division by zero and any checked lowering wait for B2 and its own ARCHITECTURE_DECISION. A Q0 probe cell that contradicts a premise keeps its form refused. Not taken: opening arithmetic in B1, or writing a follow-the-target zero-divisor rule now. |
| D78.03 SQLite target identity and qualification | The SQLite target is `{family: "sqlite", release: "3.53.1"}`, together with the exact source id and compile options that `QUALIFIED_SQLITE` records. A dialect-owned copy of that identity gates qualification. It lands in S03 with its first caller (IMPLEMENTATION_FREEDOM: B1 has no production caller), and the job store's constant is not imported. Qualification runs in process with the standard-library `sqlite3`, on `:memory:` or test-owned files with `STRICT` fixtures, after Gate 0. Its oracles are Pietto-owned expected results written before the run, the pure BAG/NULL oracle, and byte replays of existing PostgreSQL outputs. It adds a runner-acceptance domain row of its own. These in-process target runs are authorized under this Gate 1. | A version alone is not an identity. Every other build is unqualified: hosted CI asserts the refusal, and byte tests run everywhere. No Docker, engine lock, network access or new dependency is used. A new PostgreSQL or MySQL run needs a database authorization that Phase78 does not have. Runner-table row 3 (job-store storage) is not borrowed. The runner-acceptance row and the `development.md` section are VR2 acceptance rule paths, so the B1 batch runs Gate 2 wholly on the integration host and records no new bootstrap (PL.05; owner, 2026-10-27). Not taken: a version-only identity, and a third-party SQLite wheel. |
| D78.04 SQLite errors and adapter shape (lead ARCHITECTURE_DECISION, inform) | Single-file SQLite refusals use `PIE-B1000` with `SQLite SQL emission is not implemented for RelationIR: <name>. <reason>`, in the existing backend order. Native SQLite errors appear only in qualification tests, as native target failures named by `sqlite_errorname`. The emitter is a set of independent `src/pietto/sql/sqlite*.py` modules in the shape of `sql/mysql*.py`, with no shared adapter abstraction. | The source is the SQLite draft's Q5 and Q6. The lead states it to the owner as inform, at the latest before S02 seals. There is no SQLSTATE mapping and no new `PIE-*` code, and a new code needs the owner. No SQLite family reaches a PostgreSQL/MySQL binary default. The B2 pipeline shape is decided under AD78.03. No general dialect registry is introduced. |
| D78.05 Phase78 B1 ledger | The cumulative ceiling for Q0 and S01–S04 is recorded from sheet §3.2 as `C80/F240/D2300/S3`, including a D400 replay reserve, under PL.01. The option the owner selected when approving this Gate 1 (2026-11-10T11:30:07Z) stated another draft, `C80/F240/D1500/S3`, the SQLite draft's recommendation; the two differ only in D. Until the owner chooses, D1500 is the stop point (D77.16): the lead stops and asks when Phase78's cumulative D reaches 1500. The allocations are Q0 0/0/100/0, S01 10/30/150/1, S02 30/90/800/1, S03 25/75/600/1 and S04 10/30/250/0; their D1900 is above that stop point. D counts as in D76.17. The planning entry, charged to Q0, is the SQLite draft's 31 and the route drafting's 35 shell commands, plus Phase78's share of the shared passes (PL.06). | At any cap or stop point the lead stops and asks. No handoff or lane resets the ledger. The lead's message of 11:59Z, which said that these figures came from the approved answers, did not name the difference and is no owner choice. |

### Phase79 DuckDB B1

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D79.01 Phase79 B1 scope | The first Phase79 vertical is one local read-only query over one caller-supplied Arrow table. It is an explicit-module project with exactly one `duckdb.arrow("key")` source. The key is exact and non-empty, with no normalization or case folding. The query is a direct projection, optionally with one direct `IntField <op> param` comparison (`==`, `!=`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`) on one declared non-null `Int` parameter used once. Columns are `Int`, `Text`, `Bool` and finite `Float`, and `Text`, `Bool` and `Float` are projected only. The literal policy is `preserve` only, and results are compared as bags. The entry points are `check --project`, `emit-sql --project --dialect duckdb`, `compile_sql`, `compile_query` and `execute(route="duckdb_arrow")`. Refusals reuse existing codes (PIE-B1003, PIE-B1001, PIE-S2306, PIE-R1002, PIE-R1003, PIE-R1004) with new details. | Not included: single-file mode and single-file `--dialect duckdb`; JOINs, aggregation, windows, ORDER BY/LIMIT and set operations; other types and `bind-safe`; bundles, saved, relay and jobs; catalog access, extensions, URIs and caller settings; sqlglot; any PostgreSQL/MySQL SQL-byte or contract change. A new PIE code needs the owner. Whether `0.2.0` also needs a B2 stays the owner's decision, at the latest at the B1 audit; that B2 would add group by with count/sum/min/max, then ORDER BY/LIMIT. Source: sheet Q8 A. The mapping of refusals to existing codes is the lead's derivation from DuckDB draft G2 (lead, inform before S01 binds). |
| D79.02 DuckDB pin | `execute-duckdb = ["pyarrow==26.0.1", "duckdb==2.0.0"]`, pinned literally under PL.05. `duckdb` `2.0.0` is the latest stable release that the authorized probe found and installed. It was first uploaded on 2026-10-21. One `cp312-abi3` manylinux_2_27/2_28 x86-64 wheel serves CPython 3.12 and 3.13, with sha256 `ab51ec57d329d6f3df37c0287b55d71c3fc07bb54b61d1afed09104f953b1431`. On CPython 3.13.13 the probe found every D79.06 item at its unsafe default: replacement scans, external access, and extension autoinstall and autoload were on; the configuration was unlocked; the temporary directory was relative, with a cap of 90% of disk; threads and memory limit came from the host. It also found that `to_arrow_table()` keeps the original types but drops non-null flags. | No LTS status is claimed. Another release needs a new install probe and the owner. S00 closes the pending items offline in the probe environment after Gate 0: the `MAX_PARAMETERS` limit, setting and locking every D79.06 value with its read-back, proof that extensions, external access and temporary spill stay off, the installed metadata and origin, and `Table.nbytes`. The CPython 3.12 install comes from PK and natural CI. Source: sheet Q3 A. |
| D79.03 DuckDB execute API | `execute(route="duckdb_arrow", access=DuckDBArrowInput(name, table), premise=None, ...)`. `DuckDBArrowInput` is frozen, refuses copy and pickle, holds one non-empty `str` name and one `pyarrow.Table`, and performs no I/O. `premise=None` is accepted only for `duckdb_arrow`, which changes the PIE-R1001 rule text. `isolation` accepts only `"stable"`, which means the owned capture snapshot, and any other value is PIE-R1002. The input name must equal the contract `relation.name` and the connector argument (D76.06). | There is no `DuckDBInProcessPremise`, no `execute_local(...)`, and no change to `ExecutionLimits`. The PostgreSQL and MySQL routes keep their required premises. Source: sheet Q8 A. |
| D79.04 Arrow input capture | Before any session, the input is admitted only if it has at most 65,536 rows and at most 16 MiB, as measured by `pyarrow.Table.nbytes` before copying. This is a fixed private policy. The Arrow types are int64, string, bool and float64 only; dictionary, large_string, string_view, extension and null types are refused. A non-null compiled field requires `null_count == 0`. NaN and ±Inf are refused, and `-0.0` is admitted under the `Float` value contract. Text must be valid UTF-8. The capture is an owned immutable copy that drops unselected backing. Refusals are PIE-R1002 `DUCKDB_ARROW_CAPTURE_LIMIT` and `DUCKDB_ARROW_SCHEMA`. | The measure is a logical admission measure, with no physical or peak-memory guarantee. A `Text` value with NUL is refused until the owner decides otherwise, as PIE-R1006 does for bound values; the latest point is before the B1 batch Gate 2. Sources: sheet Q8 A and DuckDB draft G8. Sheet Q8 asks only that the handling of `-0.0` be stated, so its admission is the lead's derivation (lead, inform before S01 binds). |
| D79.05 DuckDB emission contract | The contract target is `{"family": "duckdb", "release": "2.0.0"}` (D79.02). A source has `scan: "arrow_registration"`. `MAX_PARAMETERS` has a DuckDB-specific value, still pending S00. A pin upgrade changes the target, and an old contract is then BLOCKED with PIE-B1003, as for another release today. | This is a public compatibility change of the emission contract. The PostgreSQL and MySQL contract values are unchanged. Source: sheet Q8 A. |
| D79.06 DuckDB session hardening | Each attempt gets one private `:memory:` session. Extensions are never installed or loaded automatically, external access is off, and replacement scans that read caller frames are off. `threads` and `memory_limit` are explicit, and nothing spills to a temporary directory. The configuration is locked and read back before the first statement. | If any item cannot be proven on `2.0.0`, the route is refused. There is no session pool, cache or reuse. This is a high-risk trust choice. Source: sheet Q8 A. |
| D79.07 DuckDB qualification | An in-process native matrix runs on the integration host, with no Docker and no engine lock, under a new domain row of validation runner acceptance. Natural CI installs the pinned duckdb into the `arrow-ready` environment through `package_smoke --extra-env`, so it holds DuckDB native evidence. The arrow-ready hash allowlist `ci/phase67-arrow-compatibility-requirements.txt` changes in the same commit. | This makes no claim of parity across databases. It does not borrow the database exception or runner-acceptance rows 3 and 4. S06 edits `development.md` and the runner-acceptance specification, and S07 may edit `ci.yml`; all three are VR2 acceptance rule paths, so their batch follows PL.05: its whole Gate 2 runs on the integration host, and no new bootstrap is recorded (owner, 2026-10-27). Sources: sheet Q3 A and DuckDB draft G7 A. |
| D79.08 Phase79 B1 ledger | The cumulative ceiling is recorded from sheet §3.2 as `C120/F360/D4100/S4`, including a D800 replay reserve, plus half of PK (PL.05). The option the owner selected when approving this Gate 1 (2026-11-10T11:30:07Z) named no figure: it read, translated, "the limited vertical slice, dependency pin and budget of the proposal's A option", which the lead wrote from the drafts' questions before reading §3.2. The lower plausible reading of it (lead, inform) is the DuckDB Gate 1 proposal's `C120/F360/D1800/S4`, which is also the DuckDB draft's budget option A ("as the proposal"); the draft recommended `D3000`, and C, F and S agree in all three. Until the owner chooses, D1800 is the stop point (D77.16): the lead stops and asks when Phase79's own cumulative D, without its half of PK, reaches 1800. D counts as in D76.17. The midpoint falls after the S03 candidate and before native qualification. The planning entry is the DuckDB draft's 33 and the route drafting's 20 shell commands, plus Phase79's share of the shared passes (PL.06). | This is not Phase77's ledger. No handoff or lane resets it. At any cap or stop point the lead stops and asks. Sources: sheet §3.2 and the selected option; the proposal and the drafts are owner-held working records outside Git. |
| AD79.01 DuckDB failure ownership (lead ARCHITECTURE_DECISION, inform) | DuckDB runtime errors become the existing `AttemptFailed`/`Failure` with phase, type name, `sqlstate=None` and `vendor=None`, as `MySQLFailure` does. An interrupt counts as cancellation. An overflow is a native target failure, with no new PIE promise. | This comes from DuckDB draft G5 and was not on the owner's sheet.
