### Phase80 interoperability

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D80.01 Phase80 scope | Phase80 delivers five one-batch adapters in `pietto.interop` and the Python user surface of D80.02, and nothing else. It runs beside Phase77 under PL.01 and PL.02, on its own ledger (D80.07). | Phase80 consumes the missing Phase77 audit's handoff at its first replay after that audit is published, and at S06 at the latest. Sources: sheet Q1 A and Q6 A. |
| D80.02 Interop API and Python user surface | `pietto.interop` exports `to_pandas(batch)`, `to_polars(batch)`, `to_numpy(batch, column)`, `to_scipy_csr(batch, row, column, value, shape)`, `plot_line(batch, x, y, *, ax)` and `plot_scatter(batch, x, y, *, ax)`. Each takes one open, caller-owned `ResultBatch` and positional field ordinals, never closes the batch, and returns a target object that stays usable after the batch closes. `to_scipy_csr` returns a canonical `scipy.sparse.csr_array` that keeps explicit zeros. The Python user surface is the existing alpha `pietto.compiler` and `pietto.execution` entry points, unchanged, plus `pietto.interop`, its five extras and the guide page `docs/interop.md`. | The surface is alpha until the Phase82 freeze. There is no compile, execute, iterate, save or whole-result helper, no `**kwargs`, and no zero-copy, allocator, mutability or thread-safety promise. Not taken: `csr_matrix`. Source: sheet Q6 A. The wording of the user surface answers Q6's "define it" and is the lead's (lead, inform before S01 seals). |
| D80.03 Interop type admission | pandas and Polars admit these flat types, nullable or not: Int (int16/32/64), Bool, Float (float64), Text (string, large_string), Decimal as decimal128 with precision up to 38, Date (date32) and zone-less Timestamp (timestamp[us]). pandas uses `pandas.ArrowDtype` of each field's exact type. Polars uses the route's mapping table, and any other output dtype is a refusal. NumPy, SciPy and Matplotlib admit only fields that the schema declares non-nullable. NumPy takes one Int, Bool or Float column. SciPy takes three distinct Int columns, with each shape dimension at most 4096. Matplotlib takes Float columns, or Int columns whose values all lie within ±2**53. | UUID, decimal256 and nested fields are refused everywhere. Nullability is read from the schema, not from one batch's values. Float finiteness is guaranteed upstream and is not an adapter refusal. NumPy returns an owned copy (D67.27). A row that the selected-pin matrix does not prove stops for the owner; it is never narrowed or cast silently. The probe covered the int64, bool, float64, string, decimal128(10,2), date32 and timestamp[us] rows at the D80.05 pins on CPython 3.13.13. The remaining rows are P0b cells after Gate 0. Not taken: the proposal's narrow table, and widening all five libraries. Source: sheet Q6 A. |
| D80.04 Interop errors | Only standard Python exceptions are used. `TypeError` covers a wrong argument type, and the existing `HandleMisuse` PIE-R4002 covers a closed batch. `ValueError` covers ordinal, admission, shape, coordinate and envelope failures. `ModuleNotFoundError` covers a missing extra, with the batch left open. Target-library failures are not wrapped. | There is no new PIE family and no diagnostics-spec change. The check order is the lead's implementation choice; the route fixes it and S01 tests it. Not taken: new PIE-R codes. Source: the Phase80 proposal, with the sheet's reclassification of ordering. |
| D80.05 Interop extras, pins and installed CI cells | Under PL.05 there are five extras. Each pins only its target library, exactly at the release the authorized probe found and installed: `interop-pandas` pins `pandas==3.1.1`; `interop-polars` pins `polars==2.1.0`, which itself pins `polars-runtime-32==2.1.0`; `interop-numpy` pins `numpy==2.5.4`; `interop-scipy` pins `scipy==1.18.1`; `interop-matplotlib` pins `matplotlib==3.11.2`. There is no combined extra and no NumPy co-pin, and `uv.lock` locks the closures. Installed witnesses run in isolated environments that `scripts/package_smoke.py --extra-env` builds, one per interop extra in each CPython job. That gives ten cells: `pietto[arrow,interop-X]` on CPython 3.12 and 3.13. | The probe resolved one 17-pin closure, identical for cp312 and cp313. It keeps PyArrow `26.0.1` and needs at most manylinux_2_28. The probe installed it on CPython 3.13.13 only. A route extra combined with an interop extra is claimed only under the stated same-PyArrow assumption and gets no cell of its own. Target versions are not checked at run time, and the documentation states that only the pins are qualified. A missing target fails in the environment that should provide it. Not taken: literal PyArrow pins in each interop extra, and an `interop` profile and pytest shard. Source: sheet Q3 a–d A. |
| D80.06 Phase80 deferred candidates | S01–S06 exclude `compile_queries(...)` (D75.03), database-native array and JSON columns and the `postgres_adbc` nested route (D76.11), `round` of a `Float` with digits (D76.05), pandas and Polars nesting, and the Arrow data-analysis example (J25). | Each keeps the latest point that the roadmap and the freeze ledger give it. `compile_queries` waits for the forged-compiled trust decision, nesting waits for Phase77 S24, and the example waits for Phase79 B1 and Phase80 S02. A later Phase80 Gate 1 must open each one. Source: sheet Q6 A. |
| D80.07 Phase80 ledger | The cumulative ceiling is `C120/F360/D4200/S10`, including a D800 replay reserve, plus half of PK (PL.05). D counts as in D76.17, and the probe and design lines count. The planning entry is the Phase80 draft's 34 and the route drafting's 26 shell commands, plus Phase80's share of the shared passes (PL.06). | The ceiling is reviewed at the midpoint, and at any cap the lead stops and asks. No lane or handoff resets it. From the approval on Phase80 spends nothing of Phase77's ledger (PL.06) and resets nothing there. Source: sheet §3.2. |
| AD80.01 `pietto.interop` boundary | `pietto.interop` is a new top-level package in the product architecture's explicit adapter plane. It depends only on the public `ResultBatch`, PyArrow and its target library. It imports none of them at package import, and it imports no `_project` owner. | It is separate from the private `pietto._project.project_arrow_interop`. Not taken: adapters inside `pietto.execution`, which would make imports heavier and mix the execution and adapter owners. Source: sheet Q6 (ARCHITECTURE_DECISION). |

## Phase77–80, editor and extension — owner decisions (2026-11-11)

On 2026-11-11 at 09:27:08Z the owner wrote (translated) "Docker Desktop is
installed. Item 6: B; everything else as you recommend", answering the lead's
explained decision sheet. The sheet gathered the questions and informs left
open while the owner was away (D77.16) into 23 numbered items, each with its
options and the lead's recommendation. It is an owner-held working record
outside Git, so each row below states its answer in full. Item 6 is the only
answer that differs from the recommendation. Where a recommendation left a
choice open, the row states the narrower reading (lead's reading). A row
revises an earlier row only where it says so, and the earlier rows keep their
text.

### Phase77

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D77.18 Budget | Raise the cumulative Phase77 ceiling to `C280/F850/D35000/S12`; only D changes. A slice that opens MySQL shapes keeps five independent review lanes in each review round, and a batch that changes only documentation gets one. | Sheet item 3, option B, recorded when the lead's conservative cumulative D was about 19,160 of 25,000. It revises D77.05's D25000 and the figure that PL.01 quotes. C, F and S, the counting rules (D76.17) and every historical cost and failure stay unchanged; reaching any ceiling still stops the phase for the owner. Fewer review lanes for a slice that opens MySQL shapes need the owner. Not taken: keeping D25000, D45000 or more, three review lanes, and pausing new slices. |
| D77.19 Native qualification runtime | Wave A's two-database qualification campaigns run on rootless podman, as their preregistration states, and their evidence may be the basis for opening the Wave A forms on MySQL (D74.09). Every other qualification framework (among them S09, S13, S15, S21 and the numeric re-run) and the repository's conformance facility (`acquire()` in `tests/_pietto_target_conformance_resources.py`) use the Docker Engine that Docker Desktop provides, which the owner has installed. Each run records the identity of its runtime. | Sheet item 1, option F. No framework or facility is changed to accept podman, and a Wave A campaign keeps podman when the Docker Engine is also reachable. A framework that needs the Docker Engine runs only when its preflight observes the Engine and otherwise keeps refusing to run. S13 keeps its stricter endpoint check, which requires the Engine component and refuses a podman record. D77.02's pinned images, offline profile, engine locks, owned cleanup and preserved failures are unchanged. | D78.10 PostgreSQL replay method for S03 (lead, under D78.08) | The byte replay of the S03 cells is one campaign on the pinned PostgreSQL 18.6 image, on the Docker Engine (D77.19), in one owned container at a time with `--pull=never --network none`, under the PostgreSQL engine lock. Its manifest, expectations, judge, driver, exit codes and void rules are frozen and reviewed before each attempt. The campaign ends with its first run that ends `PASS` or `FAILED`; before that it may contain void attempts. A void attempt is kept and charged to the Phase78 ledger. When its cause lies in the frozen package or in a reviewed runtime value, the next attempt waits for a revision that passed review; otherwise it runs the same package under a new run id. A run that never started (bad arguments or an existing run directory, a tunnel module loaded, the engine lock not free) is no attempt. D78.08's two hours per campaign are the sum of all its attempts, void ones included (lead, fail closed); the frozen driver also voids a single run that passes two hours from its own start. A `FAILED` campaign runs again only after its integrity audit finds a judge defect and an erratum is written. The result lands in the Phase78 lane as `tests/fixtures/sqlite/postgres_replay_v1.json`, which `test_each_postgres_replay_binds_the_bytes_it_ran` judges on every runner with no database; like Phase78's other tests and fixtures it reaches `main` only after the `0.1.11` cut (PL.04). After a repair or rebase an entry keeps its saved rows only while its PostgreSQL bytes, fixture rows, expectation and carriers are unchanged; the others run again as a new campaign under this method, with a reviewed preregistration delta. | The lead's method record under D78.08; it adds no authority to D78.08: no MySQL run, no publication, and no PostgreSQL run outside S03's byte replays. The campaign of 2026-11-11 had two attempts. Attempt 1, preregistration v2.2, run at 16:28:55Z: `VOID`, exit 6, because the driver raised `TypeError` (`Run.event()` got two values for `name`) before `docker container create`; no container existed, cleanup was `success`, the lock was released, and it is charged. Attempt 2, v2.3 (the event name made positional-only; its review was clean), run from 17:39:05Z to 17:39:10Z: `PASS`, exit 0; all 105 entries passed, two with the declared `ZERO_SIGN` (`select-every-type`, `group-order-by-max`) and three bare-literal columns as `numeric`; the container was removed by its label. The two runs took about 1.3 s and 4.2 s, about 5.5 s in all, inside two hours under either reading. Not taken: one attempt per campaign (v2.2's wording), and a two-hour clock per run. |

### Editor core (E-C)

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ED.12 Correction to ED.09's labels | ED.09's Boundary labels all of completion's limits as the lead's IMPLEMENTATION_FREEDOM, but two come from owner decisions. ED.03 keeps ED.02's rule that requests do no parsing or compilation, so completion never starts an analysis. The option B′ that the owner chose takes the identifiers from one lexical scan of the request's open buffer, so completion reads no snapshot, disk file or other document and offers no words of other open documents. Changing either needs the owner. The lead's IMPLEMENTATION_FREEDOM under the answer is the rest: completion reads no configuration and does not wait for a pending analysis, and a document that is not open, or whose text the server refused, gets the vocabulary alone. | ED.09's text is unchanged; this row corrects only which of its limits are the owner's. ED.09's inform that C5 leaves out the word at the request's position stays the lead's IMPLEMENTATION_FREEDOM. The second-round audit of the documentation batch that records ED.09 found it. |

## Phase77 — the lead's rulings for the second `0.1.12` batch (2026-11-13)

Four rulings of the lead, made while the native runs of the second
`0.1.12` batch were judged and carried on 2026-11-13, decide how those runs
count. The runs and their working records are outside Git, so each row states
the facts it rests on. These are lead decisions within D77.50, D77.51 and the
slices' preregistered rules, not owner answers.

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D77.56 S12's MySQL expectation and its PostgreSQL half (lead) | The fifth-round run of S12's MySQL half was not accepted because its preregistration was wrong, not the product: it expected the cleanup terminal `CLOSED` of Pietto's `mysql_rows` reader witness, while the product's clean terminal for `mysql_rows` is `LOCAL_CLOSED_REMOTE_UNOBSERVED`. The lead ruled that a new freeze of the MySQL half corrects the expectation, taking the terminal from the product's own table, and that the accepted fifth-round PostgreSQL half stands with its unchanged package, since S12's acceptance judges each half by its own run and ledger (the precedent is Wave A S16's third freeze). The seventh freeze, the sixth with a larger self-check, ran once and was accepted, and the orchestrator's comparison under the MySQL preregistration's differential pairing found the seven forms paired with the design's nullability. | The failed fifth-round MySQL run is kept and opens nothing. The halves still pair only on one tree and one wheel, which both runs used. The witness check of `source`, `delivery` and `remote_source_use_end` that the product's own clean test also reads is deferred to a later freeze, since adding it changes the frozen experiment (D77.51); the accepted run's witness met all three. Not taken: rerunning the PostgreSQL half. |
| D77.57 S09's MySQL `UUID` header parameters (lead, by S09's preregistered decision table) | The third freeze of S09's candidate experiment ended `MYSQL_MISMATCH`: MySQL returned `CAST(? AS BINARY(16))` as `VARBINARY` (column type 253), as its manual states, while the preregistration and the candidate described a MySQL `UUID` output as `BINARY(16)` (254), the only type `mysql_rows` accepts for `my_uuid_bytes`. As the decision table directs for the failing type, the lead withdrew the whole MySQL `UUID` header parameter, comparison and output alike: PIE-B1003 `query_parameter_type_not_admitted_on_target` stays at it and at every typed declaration of a query that declares one, read or not, and it enters the D77.07 packet as a retained exception. This revises D77.13, whose lowering to `my_uuid_bytes` no longer applies. `Decimal(p, s)`, `Date` and `Timestamp` opened after the fifth freeze's confirmation run matched. | A MySQL `UUID` header parameter re-enters only through a new preregistered experiment whose expected type codes come from raw observation; its output first needs an owner decision on how the public description of a `UUID` output column agrees with `VARBINARY(16)` (`USER_DECISION_REQUIRED`). The third freeze's run is kept as it is. Not taken: withdrawing only the output. |
| D77.58 S13's first attempt (lead, by S13's preregistered attempts) | Attempt 1 of S13's campaign `s13c-3a3906fdbc22` was `UNQUALIFIED` with `SEED_WARNING: mysql`: the experiment's own MySQL seed table declared `TINYINT(1)`, and MySQL 8.4.12 warns (1681) on any integer display width. Its MySQL relay also kept the server session open when the client closed first, which would have failed the later executions. Both are defects of the experiment, not of the candidate, so the lead had them fixed by new freezes of the package on the same binding and compile record. Attempt 2 of the same campaign ran once and ended `READY`, which decides the campaign. | Attempt 1 is kept as it is. The other qualification packages that carry the old relay program judge under their own preregistrations whether it affects them. Not taken: counting attempt 2 as a new campaign. |
| D77.59 What D77.50's comparison sets aside (lead) | When the batch carried its runs, the promotion records of S12, S09, Wave A S16 and Wave A S10 digested each compile record without the harness's `identity` field, which names the installed Pietto, Python and packages by path and version, and Wave A's MySQL records also without their `started` and `finished` clock fields. The records of those four slices were also compared as JSON values, their keys sorted and serialized again, not as file bytes: a record holds the compiler's documents as parsed objects, so its key order is the harness's, while every string and number is compared value for value. The lead reads that `identity` field as code identity, as D77.50 reads `compatibility` and `producer`, the clock fields as no document that the compiler derives, and the key order as nothing the experiments judge, so D77.50's comparison sets all three aside; the runtime versions were checked to be the same on both sides. | Every other field of an experiment document stays in the comparison, and a run whose runtime differs still does not carry over. ## Phase77 — the lead's rulings for the third `0.1.12` batch (2026-11-14)

Seven rulings of the lead, made while the third `0.1.12` batch was assembled on
2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14, decide how its native runs count, record the three
repairs it carries and state how R2 treats the Phase77 MySQL forms. The runs
and their working records are outside Git, so each row states the facts it
rests on. These are lead decisions within D77.50, D77.51 and the slices'
preregistered rules, not owner answers.

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D77.60 Wave A S17's MySQL native length (lead) | The first run of Wave A S17's MySQL half failed 12 of its 27 forms, the four forms with a header parameter under each of their three arguments, with one reason each: the native length of the total column was 25 where the package expected 24 for a sum or difference, and 31 where it expected 30 for a product. The SQL, parameters, values, warnings, type codes and scales held, and the run's integrity passed. The preregistration was wrong, not the product: the package took the native length from the public carrier (`my_decimal(22, 2)` or `(28, 2)`, plus sign and point), while MySQL 8.4.12 types `CAST(x AS SIGNED)` as a `LONGLONG` that joins `Decimal` arithmetic as 20 integer digits, so `Decimal(9, 2)` plus or minus it is `DECIMAL(23, 2)` and times it `DECIMAL(29, 2)`. The lead ruled the length an erratum of the preregistration: the failed run is kept and replaced by nothing, the MySQL half is frozen again as a changed experiment with MySQL's own lengths, its own campaign and budget, and runs once, and the accepted PostgreSQL half, whose package did not change, stands by D77.50, as S12's did (D77.56). The new MySQL run was accepted, and the two runs pair all 22 pairs of the pack's S17 to S19 scope. | The public carriers, the SQL, the values and the pairing rules do not change; the native length is no pairing field. The pairing pack's own sentence on that length keeps its pinned bytes; the MySQL package's length governs. Not taken: rerunning the PostgreSQL half, or reading the length as a defect of the candidate. |
| D77.61 R02's seventh-freeze run (lead) | The seventh freeze's run of R02's candidate emission experiment ended `UNQUALIFIED_RECEIPT` on both halves with the one reason `variants or their order`. The lead's integrity audit found the cause in the harness: both clients wrote their record with sorted keys, while the judge reads the key order as the order in which the client ran the variants, profiles and forms, so no run of that freeze could qualify, whatever its data. An offline replay with only the key order rewritten from the recorded values judged both halves `QUALIFIED`, and no counterexample, PostgreSQL difference, bag difference, unwitnessed or incomplete form was observed. The lead ruled the run void: it is kept, void for acceptance (D74.09) and not carried (D77.50), and its time counts toward its campaign's two hours. The eighth freeze repairs only the harness (unsorted records, a wiring self-check whose records each half's own client writes, and four more mutants), a repaired experiment, not a rebind, and its run qualified both halves, matched the four forms and the PostgreSQL reference and accepted the three templates for opening. | A run is void only on an integrity audit that names its defect; a counterexample or a bag mismatch is never voided and rerun without an owner decision line, and the replay is no acceptance evidence. Not taken: carrying the replay, or changing a candidate, expectation, form, control, verdict or judge rule with the repair. |
| D77.62 R01's counterexample (lead, by R01's preregistered rule) | The third freeze of R01's experiment ended `EXISTING_DEFECT_REPAIR_REQUIRED`: on MySQL 8.4.12 two forms of class C, a `Float` `in` read of a `let` with `capture` lines in a WHERE beside a 1000-character key, returned the outer rows of -0.0 and 0.0 twice under semijoin materialization. By the preregistration's counterexample rule the repair starts and is not answered by a new run: on MySQL a `Float` `in` or `not in` read of a `let` with `capture` lines written anywhere but a SELECT item is PIE-B1003 `membership_type_pair_not_admitted` at the read, whatever the key's width, the operator or the value's nullability; each of the four readers judges it from its own input. | No diagnostic code is added and no JSON shape changes (PL.13); an uncorrelated block (class U), a SELECT item and PostgreSQL output keep their behavior, and a statement that emission refuses for another reason reports `main`'s whole blocker list (D77.37), so a statement that also holds a stage over rows without fields reports that refusal alone (D77.64). The failed run stays as the evidence. Not taken: a confirmation run, or refusing the forms that matched. |
| D77.63 A MySQL rows read after a server error (lead) | S01's sixth-freeze run stalled on three MySQL overflow cells. The cause is in `main`: the pinned `mysql-connector-python` 26.10.0 keeps the previous row when an ERR packet follows one within a `get_rows` call, so `NativeStatement.fetch` with a count above one returned that row twice and lost the error, waited for the read timeout and failed with PIE-R3003 without a SQLSTATE, PIE-R3001 or PIE-R3999. The repair reads one packet per driver call until it holds the count or the terminal packet, so the error surfaces at once as PIE-R3003 with the server's SQLSTATE. | No diagnostic code, JSON or outcome shape changes. A read that meets no server error returns the same rows as before, so the runs that D77.50 carries from trees without the repair stand. |
| D77.64 Project stages over rows without fields (lead) | The pure verifier's fidelity review found that project SQL emission wrote a WHERE over a source whose shape declares no field, and a JOIN of two such sources, as a CTE with an empty column list, which neither database parses, and reported it VERIFIED. Such a statement is now BLOCKED with PIE-B1003 `zero_column_stage_not_admitted_on_target`, once per such stage or JOIN in statement order, and on MySQL also a capturing `let` that reads such rows inside its block read. | It is reported only where the statement would otherwise be VERIFIED, so a statement that another blocker refuses keeps exactly its blockers; the R01 refusal (D77.62) is no such blocker, since it yields to every refusal of `main`, so a statement with both reports this one alone. Such a query was already not executable. `check --project` and single-file SQL do not change. |
| D77.65 S09's MySQL queries in R2 (lead, by LD8's default) | R2 refines a MySQL query of S09's typed-parameter forms as any other query, each parameter keeping its cast, and no native qualification has run those refined statements. The lead asked whether an existing `PUBLIC_R2_*` detail fits a refusal exactly (D77.24); none does, since each names another cause (a statement time, a live query, a missing coordinate, an input error or a paired form). By the default the refinement stays as the [R2 boundary](execution.md#known-limitations) states, and a new detail (`PUBLIC_R2_TYPED_PARAMETER`, or a general `PUBLIC_R2_UNQUALIFIED_FORM` that would also cover S12's `lpad` and S13's forms) is left to the owner (D77.07, D77.16). | Ordinary execution and an ordinary `save` do not change. Not taken: borrowing `PUBLIC_R2_STATEMENT_SNAPSHOT` or `PUBLIC_R2_PAIRED_FORM`, whose meanings would then be wrong. |
| D77.66 Phase77 MySQL forms in R2 (lead, until the owner answers) | A read-only survey ran 328 MySQL forms, each form that Phase77 opens on `main`, in this batch or in a later candidate, with the controls of this batch's two refusing repairs, through R2 `save_recoverable` and `recover` on MySQL 8.4.12, and an independent check reproduced it on other data. 76 are refused before any workspace access or connection with their detail: `PUBLIC_R2_PAIRED_FORM` for the Wave A paired forms, `REFINEMENT_COMPARISON_DOMAIN` for S07, `PUBLIC_R2_STATEMENT_SNAPSHOT` for S12's `now()`, `MEANING_OUTPUT` for S09's selected `Timestamp` and `REFINEMENT_EXPRESSION_BLOCK_READ` for controls that `0.1.11` already emitted. 169 are prepared and fail closed at their first page, with a `terminal` of `FAILED` and no public reason: S12's `lpad`, S13, S06's `not (a != b)`, `same_value` and `not same_value`, S01's `abs` with the S02 sums, S03, and 126 cells of the later candidate for S06's `same_value` in a JOIN `on`. 68 execute a refined statement that no native qualification has run (S05 16, S06 13, S09 25, S01 3, S15 7 and R02 4; 56 distinct statements), each with the rows `execute()` returns on the survey's data and, for S06, S15 and R02, on 63 more variants. The other 15 never reach R2, since `compile_query` refuses them. The lead ruled, until the owner decides on a new `PUBLIC_R2_*` detail (D77.65), that this batch changes no R2 behavior and adds no detail, and that the [R2 boundary](execution.md#known-limitations) states each family's class, S05 and S06 on `main` included, as the release notes of S01, S03, S15 and R02 state theirs. | A new detail is a public-contract decision of the owner (D77.07, D77.16), and no code changes. Not taken: refusing these forms in R2 with an existing detail whose meaning would be wrong, or leaving their classes unstated. |

## Phase77 — R2 and the forms Phase77 opened on MySQL (2026-11-14)

The owner answered at 06:05Z on 2026-11-14 whether R2 refuses the
MySQL forms that Phase77 opened, and the lead ruled how R2 tells those forms
while the answer was implemented. The lead's working records are outside
Git, so each row states the facts it rests on.

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D77.67 R2 refuses the MySQL forms that `0.1.11` refused | Owner, at 06:05Z on 2026-11-14, answering "1" to the lead's question of 05:50Z whether R2 should refuse the forms that Phase77 opens on MySQL at its first step, before any workspace access or connection: option 1, a new generic detail. On MySQL, `save_recoverable` and `recover` refuse a query in a form that `0.1.11` refused with PIE-R1004 `PUBLIC_R2_UNQUALIFIED_FORM` before any workspace access or connection, since no native qualification has run R2's refined statement of any of them. The question counted the 76 forms that R2 already refused before any workspace access or connection as refused at its first step. This revises D77.65 and D77.66. | Ordinary execution, an ordinary `save`, bundles, PostgreSQL R2 and R2 of a MySQL query that `0.1.11` compiled do not change; no diagnostic code is added. Not taken: refusing only S09's forms with `PUBLIC_R2_TYPED_PARAMETER` (option 2), or keeping the refinement and documenting it (option 3, the default). |
| D77.68 How R2 tells the forms that `0.1.11` refused (lead) | `compile_query` decides it by rule two (D77.37): on MySQL, when a statement's emission is VERIFIED, it derives the same statement once more as `main` does, from the same project input in memory, and R2 refuses the query unless that derivation is VERIFIED too; a derivation that fails counts as refused. The check is R2's last before the workspace, so a query that another R2 check refuses keeps that detail: a paired form, a query that reads `now()`, S07's `Float` GROUP BY keys when R2 prepares them and S09's selected `Timestamp` when its values and parameters bind. The lead reads the owner's answer as keeping those details, since the question counted the 76 forms that R2 already refused before any workspace access or connection as refused at its first step (D77.67). `recover` makes the check also when its restated values or parameters cannot bind. Rule two is now R2's boundary: every later MySQL opening must close as `main`, and its forms join the R2 test of this check. | No public API or diagnostic code is added; `compile_query`'s result, the emitted SQL and the bundle bytes do not change, and a MySQL `compile_query` whose statement is VERIFIED derives it twice. Not taken: deciding it in R2 by compiled replay, whose readers do not close the Phase77 cells as `main` does; a list of the slices' cells; or reading the project again in R2. |

## Phase77 — owner process decisions (2026-11-14)

Two answers of the owner on 2026-11-14 set when review findings hold a
push and when a fix round is reviewed again. The lead's record of them is
outside Git, so each row quotes the owner's words, with a translation.

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D77.69 Documentation-only findings do not hold a push | Owner, at about 07:00Z: "如果都是文档问题我觉得可以先推送以后再来修" (translated: "if they are all documentation issues, I think we can push first and fix them later"). A batch whose open review findings are all documentation is pushed from its reviewed head, and a later batch fixes them. The third `0.1.12` batch was pushed so from `86dafa98`: its two review lenses found no code defect at medium or above, one documentation medium, R2's documentation of the S01, S03, S15 and R02 forms, and lows, and the first commit of the fourth batch lands that fix round as it stood. | A finding that is not documentation still holds the push, and Gate 2 and natural CI still run on the pushed head. Not taken: holding the push until the documentation fix round is reviewed. |
| D77.70 No second review after fixing findings that are not real bugs | Owner, at about 07:05Z: "之后如果只检查到medium 或者 low 等不是真bug的就在修复之后不再复核，在所有工作完成之后我会让外部统一做文档复核。避免反复跑测试。" (translated: "from now on, if a review finds only medium or low issues that are not real bugs, do not review again after fixing them; when all the work is done I will have the documentation reviewed externally in one pass. Avoid running tests again and again."). When the medium or worse findings of a review round hold no real bug, its fix round is final. A real bug is a wrong product result, a crash, data loss, a security defect, a broken fail-closed refusal, an unqualified form opened or the whitelist widened, an unintended change of a diagnostic code or JSON shape, or a qualification experiment whose judgment is loosened or whose expected values, binding or self-check make its verdict untrustworthy; any high finding is one. Documentation and fragment wording, the format of records and evidence, a gap in test coverage of correct behavior and comments are not. A judge that runs no tests classifies each fix round. | The fix round of a real bug is reviewed again. Gate 2 still runs once per batch, and each step runs its affected focused tests once. The documents get one external review after all the work is done. Not taken: a review round after every fix round. |

On that commit the third activity's PostgreSQL full receipt ran once at 08:41Z, using 51 s of its 7,200 s, and ended `FULL_RECEIPT_PASSED`: 75 cases, 277 API-generated and 6 console documents, 259 VERIFIED, 15 BLOCKED and 3 INPUT_REJECTED, the three `V_join_nullsafe` kinds VERIFIED with their expected rows, and no container or network left behind. In this batch the commit stacks on R2's refusal (D77.67): materialized again there, both maps are byte-identical, and the facility's generation for PostgreSQL gives the 277 API-generated and 6 console documents byte-identical to the receipt's, from the same emission and console inputs. Only code identity differs, the four `src` files of R2's refusal, the wheel and its module digests and the console script's digest, so D77.50 carries both runs to the batch's commit. ## Phase77 — the lead's architecture record at the completion audit (2026-11-14)

The Phase77 route's third prerequisite names the `sum(Int)` strategy an
`ARCHITECTURE_DECISION`. It was drafted with S01's first candidate on
2026-11-12, and S01 and S03 implemented it; this row records it at S27 with
the alternatives and premises the draft weighed. It is the lead's decision,
stated to the owner as inform, not an owner answer.

| Decision | Selected direction | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AD77.03 MySQL `sum(Int)` strategy (lead, inform) | In S01's S02 cell (`65f75708`) and in S03's nine forms (`adff0bce`), with the arguments their release notes state, a MySQL project `sum` of an `Int` argument `x` is the guarded SUM `CASE WHEN SUM(x) > 9223372036854775807 THEN CAST(9223372036854775807 AS SIGNED) + CAST(1 AS SIGNED) WHEN SUM(x) < -9223372036854775808 THEN CAST(-9223372036854775808 AS SIGNED) - CAST(1 AS SIGNED) ELSE CAST(SUM(x) AS SIGNED) END`. In range it is the exact sum, NULL for no or only NULL values; outside signed 64 bits the `BIGINT` arithmetic of its branch raises MySQL error 1690 (SQLSTATE 22003) and no row, never a clamped value. Every other MySQL `sum(Int)` keeps PIE-B1004 `sum_int_aggregate_range_proof_missing`, and PostgreSQL keeps its existing lowering. | Premises: MySQL's exact `DECIMAL` accumulation of `SUM` is not itself a Pietto `Int`; value, NULL and overflow follow the signed 64-bit contract, and the overflow is a native target failure with no new `PIE-*` promise, so nothing observable changes and the decision does not escalate to the owner. Evidence: the numeric R4 re-run (D77.15) matched the guard's safe and overflow cells on the text and prepared routes beside the PostgreSQL reference, with no partial delivery, and the emitted-SQL runs of S01 and S03 matched on both databases. Not taken: `CAST(SUM(x) AS SIGNED)`, which clamps an out-of-range sum; widening `Int` to `Decimal`; a reader-side range check of a `DECIMAL` result; and keeping the refusal everywhere. A wider `sum(Int)` form needs its own run and enters the D77.07 packet until then (D77.16). |
